We design specialized protocols to efficiently prove the correctness
of each operator used in transformer-based LLMs, such as GPT-2                                                                                                             Aggregate claims                                                                 Concat proofs

and Gemma 3. Recall from Section 3.1 that we certify the correct-                                                                              Proving
ness of the entire output sequence by performing inference on the
concatenated input and output tokens. Since this generates multiple                                                                                                                    Claim                                                                      Proof

predictions in a single forward pass, we can leverage batched vari-                                                                             Figure 2: System Architecture of DeepProve
ants of our protocols to prove the correctness of multiple outputs.

                                                                                                                    5
Quantization. This step of pre-processing takes a model with
weights in floating-point representation (e.g., FP32) and first con-
verts them to 𝑞-bit signed integer representation (e.g., in range
[−2𝑞−1 + 1; 2𝑞−1 − 1]) using affine quantization (see Section 2.1).
   Our system uses a symmetric quantization centered around zero
for both weights and activations, agnostic to the choice of quan-
tization bit length. Thus, we can easily adapt our system to use
different bit lengths (e.g., 8-bit, 12-bit, or 16-bit) as needed. Specifi-
cally, we perform post-training static quantization to convert the
model weights from floating-point to signed integer representa-
tion [SSZ21]. To quantize the model we need to compute the scaling                          Figure 3: Operator proving in DeepProve
factors for the weights and activations, for which we use a calibra-
tion dataset consisting of representative inputs to the model. We                 Thus, at the end of this step, we have commitments to the model
evaluate the model using the calibration dataset and collect the acti-            weights and quantization scaling factors that will be used both by
vation and weight statistics for each layer as well as the input/output           the prover and verifier.
of each layer. Using these statistics, we compute the scaling fac-
tors for activations, weights and input/output in each layer of the               4.2    Proving

                      2𝑟 · 𝑋𝑞,𝑗,𝑖 − 𝑛𝑖=1 𝑞,𝑗,𝑖                                        Avoid requantization when possible. Even if the output of a
                                         𝑛      1
          |𝑁𝑞,𝑗,𝑖 | ≤              𝑟
                                               + .                                    multiplication between two 8-bit quantized integer gives a 16-bit
                                  2             2                                     integer, this may be acceptable in some scenario. A matrix 𝑁 × 𝑀
                                                                                      matrix multiplied by a 𝑀 × 𝑃 matrix can yield at maximum an
                                                                                      integer of range 𝐵 = 2 ∗ 𝑞 + 𝑙𝑜𝑔2 (𝑀) + 1 bits. If 𝐵 < |F| or is
                                                                                      sufficiently small to still be used during fixed point multiplication
                                                                                      for non linear layers, then there is no need to requantize right after.
                                                                                      Requantizing incurs accuracy loss and thus avoiding it increases
                                                                                      the accuracy. This strategy is even more effective when using a
                                                                                      large field F during proving.
